Well, um... not really, no. I mean, everyone has a little stress during the day, especially managing a team, but I wouldn't say I'm anxious or upset when I get home. Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm racing through it or anything like that. I'm not, you know, shoveling it in.

Ashamed? Oh... well, no, I wouldn't use that word. 'Ashamed' sounds so... severe, I suppose? Like I've done something terribly wrong or committed a crime.

I've been having a little bit of trouble in the evenings. It's probably nothing too terrible, but, well, once I start eating at night, it feels almost like I can see myself doing it. And I'm telling myself to just stop, but my hands just sort of keep going back for more. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that clearly enough. Does that make sense, or is that too strange of a way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating at night?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not exactly. I mean, I wouldn't want to make it sound worse than it is, but... no, I don't feel in control. It's just like I was saying, it's almost like I'm watching someone else do it. I'm sitting there, and in my head, I'm saying, \"Okay, that's enough now, time to put it away,\" but... my hands just keep reaching for more food. \n\nIt's a bit embarrassing to admit at my age, honestly. You'd think I'd have better willpower by now. I hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill, but... does that sound like a lack of control to you?\n\nDoctor: Is this loss of control something that you experience only when eating, or do you feel this way in other situations as well?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. It really is just... just the eating. \n\nI actually like to think I'm a pretty organized person otherwise. I manage a whole team of engineers at work, and you really have to stay on top of things to keep the projects running smoothly, you know? So I'm usually very disciplined during the day. It's just... well, just this one silly area in the evenings that seems to get away from me. Everything else in my life is perfectly fine, I promise. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope it doesn't sound too strange that it only happens with the food.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating at night and feel like you're not in control, do you end up eating a lot of food?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I suppose \"a lot\" can mean different things to different people, right? But... yes. If I'm being perfectly honest—and I do want to be helpful here—it... it is quite a bit. \n\nIt's a little hard to admit, honestly. But... yes. If I'm being perfectly honest—and I do want to be helpful here—it... it is quite a bit. \n\nIt's a little hard to admit, honestly. Some evenings, I’ll just start with some leftovers from the fridge. Just a sensible bite. But then... well, I might end up making a few sandwiches, and then I'll pour some cereal, maybe have some cookies... really just whatever snack food is sitting around in the pantry. \n\nWhen I look back at it, it's far more than I would ever actually put on a plate for a normal supper. I hope you don't think I'm just completely gluttonous for doing that. Does that give you the information you needed?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed, anxious, or upset before these evening eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not really, no. I mean, everyone has a little stress during the day, especially managing a team, but I wouldn't say I'm anxious or upset when I get home. I try to keep a pretty positive attitude, honestly. I don't want to sound like someone who complains about every little thing in their life. \n\nIt usually just happens when I finally sit down to relax for the night. I'll be feeling perfectly fine, just unwinding, and then... well, the eating just sort of starts. \n\nI know some people eat because they're sad or having a hard time, but I really don't think that's me. I hope it's okay that I don't have a better explanation for it... is it normal to not feel upset beforehand?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these evening eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, um... I don't know if I'd use the word guilty, exactly. That sounds a bit... well, a bit dramatic, maybe? \n\nI mean, I certainly don't feel great about it. I suppose I feel a little silly, or maybe just a bit disappointed in myself for overdoing it when I know better. But I really try not to dwell on it. I just clean up the kitchen and tell myself tomorrow is a new day, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound careless or like I'm not taking this seriously. I just try not to beat myself up too much over it... is that an okay way to handle it, do you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're eating really quickly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm racing through it or anything like that. I hope it doesn't sound too strange that it only happens with the food.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating at night and feel like you're not in control, do you end up eating a lot of food?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I suppose \"a lot\" can mean different things to different people, right? But... yes. If I'm being perfectly honest—and I do want to be helpful here—it... it is quite a bit. \n\nIt's a little hard to admit, honestly. Some evenings, I’ll just start with some leftovers from the fridge. Just a sensible bite. But then... well, I might end up making a few sandwiches, and then I'll pour some cereal, maybe have some cookies... really just whatever snack food is sitting around in the pantry. \n\nWhen I look back at it, it's far more than I would ever actually put on a plate for a normal supper. I hope you don't think I'm just completely gluttonous for doing that. Does that give you the information you needed?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed, anxious, or upset before these evening eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not really, no. I mean, everyone has a little stress during the day, especially managing a team, but I wouldn't say I'm anxious or upset when I get home. I try to keep a pretty positive attitude, honestly. I don't want to sound like someone who complains about every little thing in their life. \n\nIt usually just happens when I finally sit down to relax for the night. I'll be feeling perfectly fine, just unwinding, and then... well, the eating just sort of starts. \n\nI know some people eat because they're sad or having a hard time, but I really don't think that's me. I hope it's okay that I don't have a better explanation for it... is it normal to not feel upset beforehand?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these evening eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, um... I don't know if I'd use the word guilty, exactly. That sounds a bit... well, a bit dramatic, maybe? \n\nI mean, I certainly don't feel great about it. I suppose I feel a little silly, or maybe just a bit disappointed in myself for overdoing it when I know better. But I really try not to dwell on it. I just clean up the kitchen and tell myself tomorrow is a new day, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound careless or like I'm not taking this seriously. I just try not to beat myself up too much over it... is that an okay way to handle it, do you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're eating really quickly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm racing through it or anything like that. I'm not, you know, shoveling it in. \n\nI suppose I might eat a little faster than if I were sitting at a nice restaurant with company, but I think that's just because I'm by myself and... well, just trying to get a quick snack before bed. I try to chew my food properly and all that. I certainly don't want to give the impression that I have terrible table manners when I'm alone. \n\nI haven't really timed myself, to be honest. I just sort of eat at a normal, steady pace, I think. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since these evening eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my weight has been pretty stable, I think. I mean, I try to keep an eye on it, naturally. I haven't noticed any big swings or anything concerning. \n\nI might fluctuate a pound or two here and there, but I think that's just normal for a guy my age, right? I try to stay reasonably active and I really don't want to let myself go. So, no, nothing drastic. I hope that's a good thing? I mean, it's better that my weight isn't all over the place, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel ashamed after these episodes of eating at night?\n\nPatient: Ashamed? Oh... well, no, I wouldn't use that word. 'Ashamed' sounds so... severe, I suppose? Like I've done something terribly wrong or committed a crime. \n\nI mean, sure, I know it's probably not the best habit for a man my age to be snacking so late, but it's really just a minor indulgence. I try to keep things in perspective. I certainly don't sit there feeling ashamed of myself. I just finish up, brush my teeth, and head to bed. \n\nI think it's better to just move on rather than dwelling on negative feelings like that, don't you agree? I hope that's the right way to look at it... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the kind of answers that are helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these evening eating episodes start?\n\nPatient: Hungry? Well, um... I'm not entirely sure, to be perfectly honest. I haven't really paid close attention to whether my stomach is actually rumbling or anything like that. \n\nI suppose I must be at least a little hungry, right? It just feels more like... well, just part of winding down after a long day managing things at work. A bit of a routine to relax. I don't think it's this overwhelming, ravenous feeling or anything out of the ordinary. \n\nI hope that's a helpful answer? I'm trying my best to give you the exact details you're looking for, I just... I haven't really stopped to analyze the exact feeling right before I go to the kitchen. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating at night when you're feeling bored?\n\nPatient: Bored? Oh, um... well, maybe sometimes? I mean, I wouldn't say I'm sitting there twiddling my thumbs or anything like that. It's more about relaxing than just eating out of pure boredom, if that makes sense? Feel bad? Oh, goodness no. I mean, well... maybe just a tiny bit full sometimes, but certainly not bad about myself. 

I'm a grown man, you know? I've worked hard my whole life, managed big projects, raised a family... having a bit of extra food in the evening isn't some sort of moral failing, is it? I wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting there beating myself up over a few snacks. That would be a bit dramatic, wouldn't it?

I just... well, I might think, "Oh, I shouldn't have had that last piece," but it's just a fleeting thought. Nothing serious. I really try to keep a positive outlook on things. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I'm just trying to be as helpful as I can.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Keep it private? Oh, goodness, no. I mean... well, like I was saying a moment ago, if my wife happens to be in the kitchen, that's perfectly fine. I don't wait for her to turn her back or go upstairs before I open the pantry. 

I'd really hate for you to get the impression that I'm being deceptive with my family. I've always prided myself on being a straightforward guy—you kind of have to be when you're managing engineering teams, right? You have to be honest and set a good example. So, no, there's no grand secret to any of it. I just have my snack, wash my plate, and that's that. 

I hope that clears it up? I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Criticize myself? Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say I do that. I think we touched on this just a moment ago, but I really don't sit there calling myself names or anything of that sort. That wouldn't be very productive, would it? 

Sure, I might have a passing thought like, "Well, you probably didn't need that extra handful of crackers," but it's... it's just a practical observation, really. Nothing harsh. When you manage people for a living, you learn pretty quickly that harsh criticism doesn't fix anything, and I try to apply that to myself as well. I've always believed in keeping things in perspective. 

Am I explaining that alright? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the answers you're looking for.

Does that make sense, or is that too strange of a way to put it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control when you're eating at night?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not exactly. I mean, I wouldn't want to make it sound worse than it is, but... no, I don't feel in control. It's just like I was saying, it's almost like I'm watching someone else do it. I'm sitting there, and in my head, I'm saying, \"Okay, that's enough now, time to put it away,\" but... my hands just keep reaching for more food. \n\nIt's a bit embarrassing to admit at my age, honestly. You'd think I'd have better willpower by now. I hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill, but... does that sound like a lack of control to you?\n\nDoctor: Is this loss of control something that you experience only when eating, or do you feel this way in other situations as well?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. It really is just... just the eating. \n\nI actually like to think I'm a pretty organized person otherwise. I manage a whole team of engineers at work, and you really have to stay on top of things to keep the projects running smoothly, you know? So I'm usually very disciplined during the day. It's just... well, just this one silly area in the evenings that seems to get away from me. Everything else in my life is perfectly fine, I promise. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope you don't think I'm just completely gluttonous for doing that. I mean, everyone has a little stress during the day, especially managing a team, but I wouldn't say I'm anxious or upset when I get home. I try to keep a pretty positive attitude, honestly. I don't want to sound like someone who complains about every little thing in their life. \n\nIt usually just happens when I finally sit down to relax for the night. I'll be feeling perfectly fine, just unwinding, and then... well, the eating just sort of starts. \n\nI know some people eat because they're sad or having a hard time, but I really don't think that's me. I hope it's okay that I don't have a better explanation for it... is it normal to not feel upset beforehand?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these evening eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, um... I don't know if I'd use the word guilty, exactly. That sounds a bit... well, a bit dramatic, maybe? \n\nI mean, I certainly don't feel great about it. I suppose I feel a little silly, or maybe just a bit disappointed in myself for overdoing it when I know better. But I really try not to dwell on it. I just clean up the kitchen and tell myself tomorrow is a new day, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound careless or like I'm not taking this seriously. I just try not to beat myself up too much over it... is that an okay way to handle it, do you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're eating really quickly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm racing through it or anything like that. I'm not, you know, shoveling it in. \n\nI suppose I might eat a little faster than if I were sitting at a nice restaurant with company, but I think that's just because I'm by myself and... well, just trying to get a quick snack before bed. I try to chew my food properly and all that. I certainly don't want to give the impression that I have terrible table manners when I'm alone. \n\nI haven't really timed myself, to be honest. I just sort of eat at a normal, steady pace, I think. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since these evening eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my weight has been pretty stable, I think. I mean, I try to keep an eye on it, naturally. I haven't noticed any big swings or anything concerning. \n\nI might fluctuate a pound or two here and there, but I think that's just normal for a guy my age, right? I try to stay reasonably active and I really don't want to let myself go. So, no, nothing drastic. I hope that's a good thing? I mean, it's better that my weight isn't all over the place, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel ashamed after these episodes of eating at night?\n\nPatient: Ashamed? Oh... well, no, I wouldn't use that word. 'Ashamed' sounds so... severe, I suppose? Like I've done something terribly wrong or committed a crime. \n\nI mean, sure, I know it's probably not the best habit for a man my age to be snacking so late, but it's really just a minor indulgence. I try to keep things in perspective. I certainly don't sit there feeling ashamed of myself. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the kind of answers that are helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these evening eating episodes start?\n\nPatient: Hungry? Well, um... I wouldn't want you to think I'm just aimlessly eating because I have nothing better to do. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I hope I'm not making a mountain out of a molehill, but... does that sound like a lack of control to you?\n\nDoctor: Is this loss of control something that you experience only when eating, or do you feel this way in other situations as well?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't think so. It really is just... just the eating. \n\nI actually like to think I'm a pretty organized person otherwise. I manage a whole team of engineers at work, and you really have to stay on top of things to keep the projects running smoothly, you know? So I'm usually very disciplined during the day. It's just... well, just this one silly area in the evenings that seems to get away from me. Everything else in my life is perfectly fine, I promise. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope it doesn't sound too strange that it only happens with the food.\n\nDoctor: When you're eating at night and feel like you're not in control, do you end up eating a lot of food?\n\nPatient: Well... um, I suppose \"a lot\" can mean different things to different people, right? But... yes. If I'm being perfectly honest—and I do want to be helpful here—it... it is quite a bit. \n\nIt's a little hard to admit, honestly. Some evenings, I’ll just start with some leftovers from the fridge. Just a sensible bite. But then... well, I might end up making a few sandwiches, and then I'll pour some cereal, maybe have some cookies... really just whatever snack food is sitting around in the pantry. \n\nWhen I look back at it, it's far more than I would ever actually put on a plate for a normal supper. I hope you don't think I'm just completely gluttonous for doing that. Does that give you the information you needed?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel stressed, anxious, or upset before these evening eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not really, no. I mean, everyone has a little stress during the day, especially managing a team, but I wouldn't say I'm anxious or upset when I get home. I try to keep a pretty positive attitude, honestly. I don't want to sound like someone who complains about every little thing in their life. \n\nIt usually just happens when I finally sit down to relax for the night. I'll be feeling perfectly fine, just unwinding, and then... well, the eating just sort of starts. \n\nI know some people eat because they're sad or having a hard time, but I really don't think that's me. I hope it's okay that I don't have a better explanation for it... is it normal to not feel upset beforehand?\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these evening eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, um... I don't know if I'd use the word guilty, exactly. That sounds a bit... well, a bit dramatic, maybe? \n\nI mean, I certainly don't feel great about it. I suppose I feel a little silly, or maybe just a bit disappointed in myself for overdoing it when I know better. But I really try not to dwell on it. I just clean up the kitchen and tell myself tomorrow is a new day, you know? \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound careless or like I'm not taking this seriously. I just try not to beat myself up too much over it... is that an okay way to handle it, do you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're eating really quickly during these episodes at night?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm racing through it or anything like that. I'm not, you know, shoveling it in. \n\nI suppose I might eat a little faster than if I were sitting at a nice restaurant with company, but I think that's just because I'm by myself and... well, just trying to get a quick snack before bed. I try to chew my food properly and all that. I certainly don't want to give the impression that I have terrible table manners when I'm alone. \n\nI haven't really timed myself, to be honest. I just sort of eat at a normal, steady pace, I think. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since these evening eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my weight has been pretty stable, I think. I mean, I try to keep an eye on it, naturally. I haven't noticed any big swings or anything concerning. \n\nI might fluctuate a pound or two here and there, but I think that's just normal for a guy my age, right? I try to stay reasonably active and I really don't want to let myself go. So, no, nothing drastic. I hope that's a good thing? I mean, it's better that my weight isn't all over the place, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel ashamed after these episodes of eating at night?\n\nPatient: Ashamed? Oh... well, no, I wouldn't use that word. 'Ashamed' sounds so... severe, I suppose? Like I've done something terribly wrong or committed a crime. \n\nI mean, sure, I know it's probably not the best habit for a man my age to be snacking so late, but it's really just a minor indulgence. I try to keep things in perspective. I certainly don't sit there feeling ashamed of myself. I just finish up, brush my teeth, and head to bed. \n\nI think it's better to just move on rather than dwelling on negative feelings like that, don't you agree? I hope that's the right way to look at it... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the kind of answers that are helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these evening eating episodes start?\n\nPatient: Hungry? Well, um... I'm not entirely sure, to be perfectly honest. I haven't really paid close attention to whether my stomach is actually rumbling or anything like that. \n\nI suppose I must be at least a little hungry, right? It just feels more like... well, just part of winding down after a long day managing things at work. A bit of a routine to relax. I don't think it's this overwhelming, ravenous feeling or anything out of the ordinary. \n\nI hope that's a helpful answer? I'm trying my best to give you the exact details you're looking for, I just... I haven't really stopped to analyze the exact feeling right before I go to the kitchen. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating at night when you're feeling bored?\n\nPatient: Bored? Oh, um... well, maybe sometimes? I mean, I wouldn't say I'm sitting there twiddling my thumbs or anything like that. I usually have plenty to occupy my mind with work and all. \n\nI suppose sometimes the evenings can get a little quiet, and... well, it's just nice to have a little something while I'm watching TV or reading. It's more about relaxing than just eating out of pure boredom, if that makes sense? I wouldn't want you to think I'm just aimlessly eating because I have nothing better to do. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm just trying to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how my evenings usually go.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat at night when you're alone, or are there other people around?\n\nPatient: Alone? Well, um... it's really just a mix, I suppose. Sometimes my wife is still up watching a program with me, or sometimes she's already headed up to bed. \n\nI don't really make a point to wait until I'm by myself or anything like that, if that's what you mean. It's just... well, whenever I happen to be in the kitchen getting a little snack. I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm sneaking around my own house! That would be a bit silly for a man my age, wouldn't you agree? \n\nIt's just a normal evening routine, whoever happens to be in the room. I hope that answers your question properly? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the clearest picture possible.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel bad about yourself after these episodes of eating at night?\n\nPatient: Feel bad? Oh, goodness no. I mean, well... maybe just a tiny bit full sometimes, but certainly not bad about myself. \n\nI'm a grown man, you know? I've worked hard my whole life, managed big projects, raised a family... having a bit of extra food in the evening isn't some sort of moral failing, is it? I wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting there beating myself up over a few snacks. That would be a bit dramatic, wouldn't it?\n\nI just... well, I might think, \"Oh, I shouldn't have had that last piece,\" but it's just a fleeting thought. Nothing serious. I really try to keep a positive outlook on things. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I'm just trying to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to keep your late-night eating private from your family members?\n\nPatient: Keep it private? Oh, goodness, no. I mean... well, like I was saying a moment ago, if my wife happens to be in the kitchen, that's perfectly fine. I don't wait for her to turn her back or go upstairs before I open the pantry. \n\nI'd really hate for you to get the impression that I'm being deceptive with my family. I've always prided myself on being a straightforward guy—you kind of have to be when you're managing engineering teams, right? You have to be honest and set a good example. So, no, there's no grand secret to any of it. I just have my snack, wash my plate, and that's that. \n\nI hope that clears it up? Nothing harsh. When you manage people for a living, you learn pretty quickly that harsh criticism doesn't fix anything, and I try to apply that to myself as well. I've always believed in keeping things in perspective. \n\nAm I explaining that alright? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the answers you're looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating at night?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, no, I wouldn't use a word like guilty. Like realizing I'll probably be a bit too full when I try to go to sleep. But guilt? No, I don't think so. Enjoy it? Oh, well... um, yes, I suppose I do. I mean, it's food, isn't it? It tastes perfectly fine. 

I wouldn't say it's some grand culinary experience or anything. It's just... you know, a bit of comfort. Something familiar at the end of a long day. I'll be feeling perfectly fine, just unwinding, and then... well, the eating just sort of starts. \n\nI know some people eat because they're sad or having a hard time, but I really don't think that's me. I haven't noticed any big swings or anything concerning. \n\nI might fluctuate a pound or two here and there, but I think that's just normal for a guy my age, right? I try to stay reasonably active and I really don't want to let myself go. So, no, nothing drastic. I hope that's a good thing? I mean, it's better that my weight isn't all over the place, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel ashamed after these episodes of eating at night?\n\nPatient: Ashamed? Oh... well, no, I wouldn't use that word. 'Ashamed' sounds so... severe, I suppose? Like I've done something terribly wrong or committed a crime. \n\nI mean, sure, I know it's probably not the best habit for a man my age to be snacking so late, but it's really just a minor indulgence. I try to keep things in perspective. I certainly don't sit there feeling ashamed of myself. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the kind of answers that are helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these evening eating episodes start?\n\nPatient: Hungry? Well, um... I'm not entirely sure, to be perfectly honest. I haven't really paid close attention to whether my stomach is actually rumbling or anything like that. \n\nI suppose I must be at least a little hungry, right? It just feels more like... well, just part of winding down after a long day managing things at work. A bit of a routine to relax. I don't think it's this overwhelming, ravenous feeling or anything out of the ordinary. \n\nI hope that's a helpful answer? Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating at night when you're feeling bored?\n\nPatient: Bored? Oh, um... well, maybe sometimes? It's more about relaxing than just eating out of pure boredom, if that makes sense? I wouldn't want you to think I'm just aimlessly eating because I have nothing better to do. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm just trying to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how my evenings usually go.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat at night when you're alone, or are there other people around?\n\nPatient: Alone? Well, um... it's really just a mix, I suppose. Sometimes my wife is still up watching a program with me, or sometimes she's already headed up to bed. \n\nI don't really make a point to wait until I'm by myself or anything like that, if that's what you mean. It's just... well, whenever I happen to be in the kitchen getting a little snack. I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm sneaking around my own house! That would be a bit silly for a man my age, wouldn't you agree? \n\nIt's just a normal evening routine, whoever happens to be in the room. I hope that answers your question properly? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the clearest picture possible.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel bad about yourself after these episodes of eating at night?\n\nPatient: Feel bad? Oh, goodness no. I wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting there beating myself up over a few snacks. I really try to keep a positive outlook on things. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? Well, um... that's a bit of a tricky question, isn't it? I mean, I suppose I start out wanting to. Like I said, it's just a bit of comfort. But... 

Well, if I'm being completely honest—and I do want to be honest with you, I know that's important—sometimes it does feel a bit... automatic? Like, once I start, it's almost as if I can see myself standing there in the kitchen, and there's a part of my brain saying, "Alright, old boy, that's probably enough for one evening." But my hands... they just sort of keep reaching for the next thing. 

It's not that I'm completely out of my mind or anything! I'm fully aware of what I'm doing. It just feels a little bit like the momentum takes over, and it's hard to put the brakes on right away. 

I really hope that doesn't make me sound weak-willed or foolish. I'm usually very disciplined in my work and everything else. Is that... does that make sense to you? Have other people described it that way?

I hope I'm answering these questions the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your weight since these evening eating episodes started?\n\nPatient: Well, um... my weight has been pretty stable, I think. I mean, I try to keep an eye on it, naturally. I haven't noticed any big swings or anything concerning. \n\nI might fluctuate a pound or two here and there, but I think that's just normal for a guy my age, right? I try to stay reasonably active and I really don't want to let myself go. So, no, nothing drastic. I hope that's a good thing? I mean, it's better that my weight isn't all over the place, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel ashamed after these episodes of eating at night?\n\nPatient: Ashamed? Oh... well, no, I wouldn't use that word. 'Ashamed' sounds so... severe, I suppose? Like I've done something terribly wrong or committed a crime. \n\nI mean, sure, I know it's probably not the best habit for a man my age to be snacking so late, but it's really just a minor indulgence. I try to keep things in perspective. I certainly don't sit there feeling ashamed of myself. I just finish up, brush my teeth, and head to bed. \n\nI think it's better to just move on rather than dwelling on negative feelings like that, don't you agree? I hope that's the right way to look at it... I just want to make sure I'm giving you the kind of answers that are helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these evening eating episodes start?\n\nPatient: Hungry? Well, um... I'm not entirely sure, to be perfectly honest. I haven't really paid close attention to whether my stomach is actually rumbling or anything like that. \n\nI suppose I must be at least a little hungry, right? It just feels more like... well, just part of winding down after a long day managing things at work. A bit of a routine to relax. I don't think it's this overwhelming, ravenous feeling or anything out of the ordinary. \n\nI hope that's a helpful answer? I'm trying my best to give you the exact details you're looking for, I just... I haven't really stopped to analyze the exact feeling right before I go to the kitchen. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating at night when you're feeling bored?\n\nPatient: Bored? Oh, um... well, maybe sometimes? I mean, I wouldn't say I'm sitting there twiddling my thumbs or anything like that. I usually have plenty to occupy my mind with work and all. \n\nI suppose sometimes the evenings can get a little quiet, and... well, it's just nice to have a little something while I'm watching TV or reading. It's more about relaxing than just eating out of pure boredom, if that makes sense? I wouldn't want you to think I'm just aimlessly eating because I have nothing better to do. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm just trying to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how my evenings usually go.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat at night when you're alone, or are there other people around?\n\nPatient: Alone? Well, um... it's really just a mix, I suppose. Sometimes my wife is still up watching a program with me, or sometimes she's already headed up to bed. \n\nI don't really make a point to wait until I'm by myself or anything like that, if that's what you mean. It's just... well, whenever I happen to be in the kitchen getting a little snack. I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm sneaking around my own house! That would be a bit silly for a man my age, wouldn't you agree? \n\nIt's just a normal evening routine, whoever happens to be in the room. I hope that answers your question properly? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the clearest picture possible.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel bad about yourself after these episodes of eating at night?\n\nPatient: Feel bad? Oh, goodness no. I mean, well... maybe just a tiny bit full sometimes, but certainly not bad about myself. \n\nI'm a grown man, you know? I've worked hard my whole life, managed big projects, raised a family... having a bit of extra food in the evening isn't some sort of moral failing, is it? I wouldn't want you to think I'm sitting there beating myself up over a few snacks. That would be a bit dramatic, wouldn't it?\n\nI just... well, I might think, \"Oh, I shouldn't have had that last piece,\" but it's just a fleeting thought. Nothing serious. I really try to keep a positive outlook on things. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for? I'm just trying to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually try to keep your late-night eating private from your family members?\n\nPatient: Keep it private? Oh, goodness, no. I mean... well, like I was saying a moment ago, if my wife happens to be in the kitchen, that's perfectly fine. I don't wait for her to turn her back or go upstairs before I open the pantry. \n\nI'd really hate for you to get the impression that I'm being deceptive with my family. I've always prided myself on being a straightforward guy—you kind of have to be when you're managing engineering teams, right? You have to be honest and set a good example. So, no, there's no grand secret to any of it. I just have my snack, wash my plate, and that's that. \n\nI hope that clears it up? I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever criticize yourself after one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Criticize myself? Nothing harsh. When you manage people for a living, you learn pretty quickly that harsh criticism doesn't fix anything, and I try to apply that to myself as well. I've always believed in keeping things in perspective. \n\nAm I explaining that alright? I just want to be sure I'm giving you the answers you're looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty after eating at night?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Oh, no, I wouldn't use a word like guilty.
